<doc> B 
 43. Demographics: The population census of 2011 estimated a total population of 5,131,902 (1,172,568 households) 
 in the GRB which is 19.4 per cent of the total pop- 
 ulation of Nepal. The GRB is home to 2,427,654 indigenous 
 people belonging to more than 40 ethnic groups, the majority of which are considered indigenous nationalities. 
 In GRB, Ethnic groups have lower literacy levels (86.3 per cent). Life expectancy of ethnic groups is 69.86 years. 
 44. Economy: Agriculture is the mainstay of the Nepalese 
 economy. The agricultural sector comprises about 20% of the total GDP and 
 contributes almost one-third of total GDP and 
 employment in the country. However, insufficient produc- 
 tion capacity has been associated with farming prac-

<doc> GREEN CLIMATE FUND FUNDING PROPOSAL V 3.0 | PAGE 17 OF 80 
 B 
 Green Climate Fund is a public-private partnership that aims to mobilize and manage 
 funds for climate change adaptation, mitigation and resilience projects in developing countries. The fund will be 
 used to support the implementation of national climate policies and strategies, as well as to 
 strengthen the capacity of local governments, communities and other stakeholders to respond to climate-related 
 hazards and disasters. Green Climate Fund is a joint venture between the Government of Nepal, the World Bank, 
 the Asian Development Bank, the United States Agency for International Development (USAID) and the 
 Asian Infrastructure Investment Bank (AIIB). </doc>

     Project components 
     Component 1: Community resilience Building climate- 
     resilient areas of the GRB through improvements in agricul- 
     tural productivity and adaptation to climate change. The project is designed 
     to improve the climate resilience of vulnerable communities, the project is designed to shift planning and implementation 
     political boundaries such as district and municipalities as a 
     re landscape along the basis becomes one unit of planning 
     structures linking all the impacted communities in the upstream 
     basin by leveraging management, land use planning, and natural resource management. The administrative

<doc> Financial and fiduciary management 
 140. Contractual arrangement and fund flow. An implementing 
 agreement will be signed between the Accredited Entity 
 (IUCN) and MOFE as the Executing Entity for this project. 
 141. Funds will be transferred from GCF to IUCN accord- 
 ing the Funded Activities Agreement (FAA) related to the 
 executing entity, with disbursement based on semi-annual work plans and discharge plan agreed between UCN and the 
 executing entity. 
 142. As an Accredited Entity, IUCN Headquarters/Asia Ex- 
 cuting Entity, with disbursement based on semi-annual work plans and discharge plan agreed between UCN and the 
 executing entity. 
 143. Accounting and financial reporting. The PMU will be 
 responsible for the accounting and fiduciary management of 
 all funds disbursed to executing entities. The PMU will be 
 prepared by PSU, in accordance with the guidelines issued by 
 procurement, staff recruitment and auditing. 
 144. Technical service for project management, including 
 management, and physical as well as financial and 
 green arrows reflect financial reporting. 
 Figure 2: Organigram of the Project </doc>

 | 147 | As an AE for GEF, IUCN has already implemented a number of projects in Guatemala, Kenya and Sri Lanka. | 
 | 148. | UCN also implemented the "Ecosystems for Resilience" project in Nepal, Pakistan and Uganda implementing a flagship project in 2016. The project helped vulnerable rural mountainous communities to implement climate change adaptation strategies. IUCN in partnership with UNDP implemented the "Adaptation: Strengthening the resilience and adaptive capacity of local and national adaptation strategies" project based on Adaptation Strategies for Nepal, Pakistan and Uganda. | 
 | 149. | UCN also implemented the "Ecosystems for Resilience" project in Nepal, Pakistan and Uganda implementing a flagship project in 2016. The project helped vulnerable rural mountainous communities to implement climate change adaptation strategies. IUCN in partnership with UNDP implemented the "Adaptation: Strengthening the resilience and adaptive capacity of local and national adaptation strategies" project based on Adaptation Strategies for Nepal, Pakistan and Uganda. | 
 | 150. | Currently, UCN in partnership with The Mountains Innovation Project in the Himalayas (Nepal), Mount Elgon (Uganda) and the Gandaki Project is the first proposal to implement climate change adaptation projects. | 
 | 151. | MOFE is the technical ministry (local ministry) for all climate-related conventions and related funds. There is a separate Ministry of Climate Change and Energy (MOCE) in Nepal, which is responsible for implementing climate change adaptation projects. The MOCE has been working closely with UNDP through its capacity enhancement project "Strengthening Capacities to Implement Climate Change Adaptation Projects" since 2013. | 
 | 152. | UCN also implemented the "Ecosystems for Resilience and Sustainable Development (ESRD) Project" in Nepal, which is a joint project between UCN and the Ministry of Natural Resources and Environment (MNRE). The ESRD Project aims to strengthen the resilience of ecosystems to climate change impacts. | 
 | 153. | MOFE has been working closely with UNDP through its capacity enhancement project "Strengthening Capacities to Implement Climate Change Adaptation Projects" since 2013. # No-objection letter issued by the national designated authority(ies) or focal point(s)

## Amenity #1: No Objection Letter from NDA Nepal

## Government of Nepal MINISTRY OF FINANCE

## SINGHADURBARI KATHMANDU, NEPAL

## To The Green Climate Fund ("GCF")

## Ret. Funding proposal for the GCF by IUCN regarding improving climate resilience of vulnerable communities and ecosystems in the Gandaki River Basin, Nepal

## Dear Madam Sir, project

## We refer to the project improving climate resilience of vulnerable communities and ecosystems in the Gandaki River Basin, Nepal.

## The Government of Nepal has no objection to the funding proposal as submitted by International Union for Conservation of Nature (IUCN) on 23 June 2018.

## We acknowledge that this letter will be made publicly available on the GCF website.

## Kind regards,

## Shreekrishna Nepal

## Ministry of Forest and Environment

## International Union for Conservation of Nature (IUCN)

## Tel: Minister 421196, Secretary 421132, International Economic Cooperation Coordination Division 421174, Fax No. 421184, 421185, Website: www.mef.gov.np

<doc> Improving Climate Resilience of Livelihoods 
 In the GIRS the situation of under nutrition and malnutrition of girls is not as severe as in far western 
 districts in Nepal, and Nepali women are generally better off than their male counterparts. The 
 pockets of poverty and crisis take place mostly including Chewa (in Ghaziabad and Chitwan), Maghi, Bote, 
 and Tharu who are generally poor, not educated in terms of nutritious food, are often subject to child 
 poverty for education beyond primary level, or her 
 Human development Index (HDI) and Human Poverty Index (HPI) 
 The Human Development Index (HDI) score for Nepal
